11472               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
11473               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
11474               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
11475               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
11476               	; 	' 7 an east one.
11477               	; 	'
11478               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
11479               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
11480               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
11481               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
11482               	; 	'
11483               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
11484               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
11485               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
11486               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
11487               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
11488               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
11489               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
11490               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
11491               	; 	'
11492               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
11493               	; 	' move at all.

11755               	;
11756               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11757               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11758               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11759               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11760               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11761               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11762               	; 	'
11763               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11764               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11765               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11766               	; 	'
11767               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11768               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11769               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11770               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11771               	; 	'
11772               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11773               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11774               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11775               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11776               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11777               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11778               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11779               	; 	'
11780               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11781               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11782               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11783               	; 	'
11784               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11785               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11786               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11787               	; scan_furn:
11788               cvb_SCAN_FURN

11830               	; 		' work: scan_escs runs immediately below and draws the same three rows
11831               	; 		' for the same three floors. Every input is a pure function of fl --
11832               	; 		' scan_base, the side lookup, the masks, scan_pat -- and scan_or1 is an
11833               	; 		' OR, so doing it twice cannot differ from doing it once. Sixteen
11834               	; 		' statements, about 320 bytes, and nine VDP writes a floor that nothing
11835               	; 		' on screen could reflect.
11836               	; 		'
11837               	; 		' It is the shape a routine takes when a feature is split out and the
11838               	; 		' original is left behind -- scan_escs is this block plus the colour
11839               	; 		' write that was added later, and it carried a copy of the explanatory
11840               	; 		' comment as well as the code.

12128               	;
12129               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
12130               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
12131               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
12132               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
12133               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
12134               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12135               	; scan_wipe:
12136               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
12137               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
12138               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
12139               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
12140               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
12141               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
12142               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
12143               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
12144               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
12145               	; 	'
12146               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
12147               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
12148               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
12149               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
12150               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
12151               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
12152               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
12153               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
12154               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
12155               	; 	'
12156               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
12157               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
12158               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
12159               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
12160               	; 	'
12161               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
12162               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
12163               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
12164 EF54 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     EF56 1680

     EF5A 20E0

12231               	;
12232               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
12233               	; 	'
12234               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
12235               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
12236               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
12237               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
12238               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
12239               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
12240               	; 	'
12241               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
12242               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
12243               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
12244               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
12245               	; 	'
12246               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
12247               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
12248               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
12249               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
12250               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
12251               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
12252               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
12253               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
12254               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
12255               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
12256               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
12257               	; 	say = 3 - elvl
12258 EFE2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ELVL,r0
     EFE4 2152

     EFEC 0003

     EFF4 21E9

12280               	; 	' 118, NOT 120, AND THE TWO HAVE TO BE WORKED OUT THE SAME WAY. A marker
12281               	; 	' is three pixels wide and the car is five, so putting both left edges at
12282               	; 	' the same x leaves their CENTRES two apart -- and an actor standing dead
12283               	; 	' centre in the lift read as standing at its left-hand edge.
12284               	; 	'
12285               	; 	' An actor in the shaft is at store x 120 on screen 3, which is canvas
12286               	; 	' 3*16 + 120/16 = 55, so his three pixels are screen 119-121 and his
12287               	; 	' centre is 120. Five pixels centred on 120 start at 118. The character
12288               	; 	' version had the same two-pixel error (char column 7, bits 248, screen
12289               	; 	' 120-124) and the sprite inherited it.
12290               	; 	SPRITE 26,sdy,118,P_RADCAR,C_RCAR

13046               	;
13047               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
13048               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
13049               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
13050               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
13051               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
13052               	; 	'
13053               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
13054               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
13055               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
13056               	; 	'
13057               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
13058               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
13059               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
13060               	; 	'
13061               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
13062               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13063               	; bonus_count:
13064               cvb_BONUS_COUNT
13065               	; 	#bval = 100

     F6E8 F63A     
13166               	;
13167               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
13168               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
13169               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
13170               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
13171               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
13172               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
13173               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
13174               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
13175               	; 	'
13176               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
13177               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
13178               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13179               	;
13180               	; do_escape:
13181               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
13182               	; 	escapd = 0
13183 F6EA 04C0  14 	clr r0
13184 F6EC D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCAPD
     F6EE 2112     
13185               	; 	rsn = 0
13186 F6F0 04C0  14 	clr r0

     F6F4 220A

     F6F8 F726

13191               	; 	' Two ways to lose a Kop here, and the message has to be read BEFORE the

13220               	; 	' THE MESSAGE BOX, ON THE GAME SCREEN AND NOT INSTEAD OF IT.
13221               	; 	'
13222               	; 	' It used to CLS before GAME OVER, so the last thing the player saw was
13223               	; 	' the store being deleted and then two words on an empty field -- which
13224               	; 	' reads as the program ending rather than as the game ending. The store
13225               	; 	' stays; the message sits in the middle of it.
13226               	; 	'
13227               	; 	' THE BOX IS THE FONT'S OWN BACKGROUND. Every font character is black on
13228               	; 	' HUD_BG (dark blue), so a row of SPACES is a solid dark blue bar. A blank
13229               	; 	' row above and below the text is a frame for the price of two strings and
13230               	; 	' no new characters, and it reads against the store because nothing else on
13231               	; 	' the playfield is that colour.
13232               	; 	'
13233               	; 	' THIRTEEN WIDE, WHICH IS THE LONGEST MESSAGE PLUS ONE EITHER SIDE.
13234               	; 	' HE GOT AWAY and THE BIPLANE are both eleven. Every string below is padded
13235               	; 	' to exactly thirteen so the box has straight edges, and column 10 puts it
13236               	; 	' at columns 10-22 -- centre 16, the screen's.
13237               	; 	'
13238               	; 	' THE REASON IS ALWAYS ON THE SAME ROW, and that is what lets one layout
13239               	; 	' serve both cases. Losing a life draws three rows around it; losing the
13240               	; 	' last one draws two more ON TOP, so GAME OVER appears above the reason
13241               	; 	' rather than replacing it. Printing the reason at a different row for each
13242               	; 	' case would need `PRINT AT` with a variable, and every other PRINT in this
13243               	; 	' program uses a constant.
13244               	; 	PRINT AT 330,"             "
13245 F72C 0200  20 	li r0,330
     F72E 014A

13407               	;
13408               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
13409               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
13410               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
13411               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
13412               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
13413               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
13414               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
13415               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
13416               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
13417               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
13418               	; 	' Harry was caught.
13419               	; snd_off:

13518               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
13519               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
13520               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is

13522               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.

13567               	; 	' A PRIZE IS AN ARPEGGIO -- testsounds PICKUP C. C5, E5, G5, then a fade
13568               	; 	' on the top note: it RISES, which is what makes it read as a reward
13569               	; 	' rather than as an event, and it ARRIVES, because a major triad resolves
13570               	; 	' where an arbitrary rise just stops.
13571               	; 	'
13572               	; 	' It was two notes struck together on two channels and held. That needed
13573               	; 	' both channels for one sound, said nothing in particular, and had no
13574               	; 	' shape at all -- a chord is a texture, and this wants to be a phrase.
13575               	; 	'
13576               	; 	' NOT THE MEASURED ONE, ON PURPOSE. The 2600 tops its pickup out at
13577               	; 	' 2543 Hz, and a square wave there is a shriek; TIA's softer timbre
13578               	; 	' carries it and the SN76489's does not. This peaks at 784 Hz. Copying the
13579               	; 	' recording would have been faithful and unpleasant, and the player earns
13580               	; 	' this sound dozens of times a round.

13753               	; 	' THE JUMP IS A WARBLE, NOT A SWEEP -- testsounds variant A, measured off
13754               	; 	' the 2600 at about 415 Hz alternating with 188 for roughly 280 ms.
13755               	; 	'
13756               	; 	' It used to be a rising sweep, 500 down to 120 in steps of 40. That was
13757               	; 	' invented rather than measured, and it had the wrong SHAPE: a sweep reads
13758               	; 	' as something departing, which is a fine idea and is not what the original
13759               	; 	' does. Eight ticks alternating is four cycles of warble, which is what the
13760               	; 	' recording shows.
13761               	; 	'
13762               	; 	' 270 is 415 Hz and 595 is 188 Hz (divisor = 3579545 / (32 * f), so a
13763               	; 	' SMALLER divisor is a HIGHER note -- the pair reads backwards from how it
13764               	; 	' is written).
13765               	; 	IF swt > 0 THEN
13766 FB60 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SWT,r0
     FB62 2094

     FB66 0100

     FB6E FBEE

13821               cv978
13822               	;
13823               	; 	' THE LOW-TIME WARNING IS SILENT. There was a beep a second under ten, and
13824               	; 	' it was the wrong instrument for the job: the last ten seconds are the
13825               	; 	' busiest part of a round, so a repeating tone arrives exactly when the
13826               	; 	' player most needs to hear the hazards, and it fought the prize arpeggio
13827               	; 	' for channel 2 as well. The flash says the same thing and says it in the
13828               	; 	' place the player is already looking.
13829               	;
13830               	; 	' FOOTSTEPS ARE NOISE, AND ON THE NOISE CHANNEL -- testsounds variant A.
13831               	; 	'
13832               	; 	' They were two alternating TONES on channel 0, which is neither what the
13833               	; 	' original does nor a good use of a channel: the recording says a footstep
13834               	; 	' is a short burst of white noise, and channel 3 was sitting unused while
13835               	; 	' the footsteps and the jump sweep shared channel 0 and had to take turns.
13836               	; 	'
13837               	; 	' TESTSOUNDS RUN D: a SHORT tick at the measured RATE.
13838               	; 	'
13839               	; 	' The two dials are independent and both were tried at the bench. The
13840               	; 	' 2600 plays a 64 ms burst every 165 ms -- six a second -- and variant A
13841               	; 	' copies both. Ten a second was tried on the way here and reads as
13842               	; 	' hurrying rather than running. What D changes is not the cadence but the
13843               	; 	' TICK: two frames rather than four, and a notch louder to carry at half
13844               	; 	' the length. Same six a second, less of it.
13845               	; 	'
13846               	; 	' EIGHT A SECOND, AND THE ACCUMULATOR NOW CARRIES ITS REMAINDER.
13847               	; 	'
13848               	; 	' sfw counted pixels and the trigger did `sfw = 0`, THROWING AWAY whatever
13849               	; 	' was past the threshold. kspd is about four pixels a pass, so a threshold
13850               	; 	' of 17 did not fire at 17 -- it fired at the first multiple past it, about
13851               	; 	' 20, and every rate this was ever set to came out slower than the number
13852               	; 	' said: the "six a second" was 5.2 and the "ten" that read as hurrying was
13853               	; 	' really 8.7. Subtracting the threshold instead keeps the fraction, so the
13854               	; 	' rate is what it claims to be at any walking speed.
13855               	; 	'
13856               	; 	' 104 px/s over 15 px is 6.9 a second -- between the 8.0 that read as
13857               	; 	' slightly hurried and the 6.1 that read as trudging. The dial is this one
13858               	; 	' constant and the subtraction below, which must stay threshold + 1 or the
13859               	; 	' carry is wrong.
13860               	; 	'
13861               	; 	' SOT = 2 IS THE SHORTEST TICK THERE IS, and 1 is SILENCE. The decay block
13862               	; 	' below runs LATER IN THE SAME sfx_tick, so a count of 1 is decremented to
13863               	; 	' zero on the pass that set it and the note-off fires before the sound has
13864               	; 	' lasted any time at all. 2 survives to the next pass, which is one pass of
13865               	; 	' sound -- about 42 ms against the bench's 33, as close as a per-pass timer
13866               	; 	' gets, and the reason the bench and the game cannot sound identical.
13867               	; 	'
13868               	; 	' Register 4 is white noise at the fastest rate.
13869               	; 	'
13870               	; 	' VOLUME 9, WHICH IS UNDER EVERYTHING ELSE ON PURPOSE. The hit, the prize
13871               	; 	' and the jump are 12 and 13, and they happen ONCE; footsteps play for the
13872               	; 	' whole round. At 13 they were level with the events they are supposed to
13873               	; 	' sit beneath, so the effects that carry information had nothing to stand
13874               	; 	' out from. The tone version was 7 for the same reason -- noise does read
13875               	; 	' quieter than a tone at the same number, which is why this is 9 and not
13876               	; 	' back to 7.
13877               	; 	IF sfw > 14 THEN
13878 FBEE D020  34 	movb @cvb_SFW,r0
     FBF0 2208     
13879 FBF2 0201  20 	li r1,3840   ; 15*256
     FBF4 0F00     
13880 FBF6 9040  18 	cb r0,r1

13882 FBFA 0460  28 	b @cv984
     FBFC FC34     
13883               cv985

     FC00 2208

     FC04 F100

     FC08 2208

14000               	;
14001               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
14002               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
14003               	; 	'
14004               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
14005               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
14006               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
14007               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
14008               	; 	'
14009               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
14010               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
14011               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
14012               	; 	'
14013               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
14014               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
14015               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
14016               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
14017               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
14018               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
14019               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
14020               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
14021               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
14022               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
14023               	; 	#if TI994A
14024               	; 	BANK 1
14025      0310     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

14163               	;
14164               	; 	' THE COLOUR TABLE, 472 bytes of the same value ($14 = black ink on dark blue).
14165               	; 	'
14166               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE COLOR 32,59,font_col, which is ONE synchronous call that
14167               	; 	' writes all three screen thirds (define_color always does the LDIRVM3 triple
14168               	; 	' copy). It replaced a VPOKE loop that wrote this one value 1,416 times paced by
14169               	; 	' 24 WAITs -- the largest scripted delay in the boot, and the reason the title
14170               	; 	' screen used to fill in visibly rather than appearing at once.
14171               	; 	'
14172               	; 	' EIGHT BYTES PER CHARACTER, not one. Supply fewer and DEFINE COLOR reads
14173               	; 	' whatever follows in ROM as colour data, with no error at build or run time.
14174               	;
14175               	; font_col:
14176               cvb_FONT_COL
14177               	; 	DATA BYTE $14,$14,$14,$14,$14,$14,$14,$14	' space

14295               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
14296               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
14297               	; 	'
14298               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
14299               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
14300               	; 	' being small sprites.
14301               	; 	'
14302               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
14303               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
14304               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
14305               	; 	'
14306               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
14307               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
14308               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
14309               	;
14310               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
14311               cvb_SPR_KELLY
14312               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00
14313 63DA 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00

15307               	;
15308               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
15309               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
15310               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
15311               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
15312               	;
15313               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
15314               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
15315               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
15316               	; 	' unreadable.
15317               	;
15318               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set
15319               cvb_SCAN_COL3
15320               	; 	DATA BYTE $EE,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EC,$EC,$EC,$BC
15321 728A EEEE     	byte >ee,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ec,>ec,>ec,>bc
     728C EEEE     
     728E ECEC     
     7290 ECBC

     7296 ECEC     
     7298 ECBC

     729C ECBC     
     729E EEEE     
     72A0 EEEE

15430               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
15431               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
15432               	; 	'
15433               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
15434               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
15435               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
15436               	; 	'
15437               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
15438               	; 	' blit is
15439               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
15440               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
15441               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
15442               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
15443               	; 	'
15444               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
15445               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
15446               	; 	'
15447               	; 	' template ids:
15448               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
15449               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
15450               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
15451               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
15452               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
15453               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF0
15454               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF1
15455               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF2
15456               	; 	'   8 T_ROOF3
15457               	; 	'   9 T_ROOF4
15458               	; 	'   10 T_ROOF5
15459               	; 	'   11 T_ROOF6
15460               	; 	'   12 T_ROOF7
15461               	; 	'   13 T_END_W
15462               	; 	'   14 T_END_E
15463               	;
15464               	; stor_tpl:	' 15 templates x 160 bytes
15465               cvb_STOR_TPL
